# MANDATORY EXECUTION ORDER

This document contains two sequential bodies of work. **Do not interleave them.**

1. **PHASE 0 — CLOSE MARION-IA-USA LLM MANAGER v0.11.** Execute the incorporated handoff below in the stated queue order. Treat the handoff's verified live state as the starting point, but re-verify before changing anything because runtime state can drift.
2. **PHASE 0 EXIT GATE.** Do not start Agent Manager work until:
   - the MIAM-00111 production topology is selected, implemented, verified, and documented;
   - the required remaining MIAM-00111 benchmark legs are complete;
   - the Phase 21 frontier-pool work is either completed or explicitly documented as BLOCKED/DEFERRED according to the handoff constraints;
   - `MARION-IA-USA-DEFAULT-MODEL-BASELINE-QUALIFICATION.md` is written;

3. **PHASE 1+ — AGENT MANAGER.** After the Phase 0 exit gate passes, continue directly into the full original Agent Manager V2 plan included after the handoff. Do not stop merely because Phase 0 completed.

## Phase 0 operating rules

- The uploaded handoff is the authoritative queue for remaining v0.11 work and explicitly supersedes the older §7 queue in `HANDOVER-v011.md`.
- Preserve the handoff's constraints exactly, especially: no RDMA/multi-node/SR-IOV work for Phase 21, do not disturb the Worker Bee rollout, preserve rollback presets, do not expose secrets, and use the established branch/repository authorization rules.
- Prefer the handoff recommendation of **TP2/DP3** for MIAM-00111 because the recorded evidence strongly favors it at the ~10-concurrent-coding-agent target. If live verification uncovers contradictory evidence, document it and make a reasoned decision rather than blindly applying a stale setting.
- Before destructive or service-changing operations, capture current state and ensure rollback is available.
- Reuse the verified access/deployment patterns in the handoff. In particular, use the established `pve.py` / `push.py` workflow rather than inventing a new remote-execution mechanism unless that path is broken.
- When benchmarking, honor the maintenance-state and single-benchmark-at-a-time rules so the recovery engine does not invalidate the run.
- Produce evidence as you go: command/config snapshots, test results, benchmark outputs, final service state, and report updates.
- **Autonomy rule:** ordinary technical choices are yours to make. Do not pause for permission unless credentials are missing, a required resource is unavailable, or an action would create material destructive/business risk.

### 3.1 Phase 20 finale: pick the MIAM-00111 topology winner, implement, document ⭐ HIGHEST PRIORITY

Both candidate topologies were fully benchmarked (8/8 legs each, C1/C4/C8/C10 × 4K/16K, 32 prompts, 256 out). Verified results from `/root/bench_00111.log` (TP2/DP3) and `/root/bench_tp4.log` (TP4) on VM103:

| Leg | TP2/DP3 (3 replicas × 2 GPU, all 6 GPUs) | TP4 (1 replica × 4 GPU, 2 idle) |

| C1@4K | TTFT 8.6s · ITL 101ms · 2,635 tok/s | TTFT 56.4s · ITL 287ms · 957 tok/s |
| C1@16K | TTFT 77.1s · ITL 184ms · 2,599 | TTFT 225.7s · ITL 738ms · 1,061 |

# PHASE 1+ — ORIGINAL AI AGENT MANAGER V2 PLAN

The content below is the original V2 autonomous plan. Its implementation begins **only after Phase 0 passes**. Where its wording says to inspect the existing LLM Manager first, use the now-closed v0.11 baseline and its final qualification report as the authoritative starting baseline.


**Document status:** V2 — incorporates product-owner answers from 2026-09-13 and current open-source research  
**Primary executor:** GLM 5.3 Flash autonomous software-engineering agent  
**Execution mode:** Build, test, document, and make decisions autonomously. Do not stop for ordinary architectural ambiguity.  
**Primary objective:** Extend the existing **LLM Manager VM/application** into a unified infrastructure-management web application that can create, configure, access, monitor, and assign work to persistent AI agents on Proxmox.  
**First operational target:** A persistent **Hermes STEA** per employee, created with less than five minutes of human input and usable from a web interface within 15 minutes target / 30 minutes maximum.  
**Longer-term target:** Harness-neutral management of Hermes, Pi, Ruflo, and OpenClaw; Jira-linked projects and plans; Frontier-model supervisors; local-model worker agents; and a fleet UI that remains usable at 100–1,000 agents.

---

# 1. Executive directive to the implementation agent

You are not being asked to produce another architecture essay. You are being asked to **modify the existing LLM Manager system and ship a working prototype**.

Use these rules:

1. **Inspect first, then modify.** Read the existing LLM Manager repository, deployment method, services, auth, reverse proxy, database, and UI before changing anything.
2. **Do not create another management VM.** Agent Manager lives on the same VM as LLM Manager and appears as additional navigation tabs/routes in the same infrastructure-management experience.
3. **Do not rewrite working LLM Manager functionality.** Preserve it. Add Agent Manager as a modular subsystem.
4. **Make decisions.** If two approaches are reasonable, choose the simpler reversible approach, record the decision in an ADR, and continue.
5. **Do not ask the human to choose libraries or patterns that you can decide yourself.** Ask only when blocked by missing credentials, unavailable infrastructure, or a destructive decision with material business risk.
6. **Build the vertical slice first.** A single Hermes STEA that can be created and chatted with from the web is more valuable than ten unfinished abstractions.
7. **Keep every change reversible.** Back up the current application/database/configuration before schema or deployment changes. Use migrations. Use feature flags for incomplete adapters.
8. **Commit often.** Create small commits with clear messages after each working milestone.
9. **Measure provisioning.** Human interaction time, clone time, boot time, configuration time, harness readiness, and time-to-first-use must be captured independently.
10. **Leave the system healthier than you found it.** Add health checks, service documentation, backup instructions, and rollback steps.

---

# 2. Product vision

The LLM Manager and Agent Manager together become the control plane for Startup Teams' local AI infrastructure.

The application should eventually answer these questions from one browser:

- Which local models are online and how much capacity is available?
- Which AI agents exist?
- Which employee or business function owns each agent?
- Which harness and model is each agent using?
- Is an agent online, idle, working, blocked, waiting for review, or unhealthy?
- What project/Jira issue is it working on?
- What plan/instructions was it given?
- What did it change?
- What is waiting for human approval?
- Which agents need attention right now?
- Which agents are using external Frontier models and incurring cost?
- Can I message an agent right now from the browser?
- Can I create another persistent STEA without manually building a VM?

The product is an **operations console first**, not a wall of chat windows.

---

Full quick matrix C1–C10 × 4K/16K all-pass, ~8.2K tok/s at 16K, soak 400/400 at C10×8K.\n- **VM103 (6× RTX 3080 20G, MIAM-00111), Qwen3.8-27B AWQ dense:** DP6 **OOMs** (each DP worker loads full 27B weights). Then quick matrix C1/4/8/10 × 4K/16K per topology; compare aggregate tok/s AND per-GPU power draw (`nvidia-smi --query-gpu=power.draw`) — fewer active GPUs at similar quality = better energy/task.\n\n## VM401 NVIDIA per-kernel driver risk\nVM401 boots kernel 6.8.0-139 but NVIDIA modules existed only for 138 (no DKMS). {"content": "1|#!/usr/bin/env python3\n2|\"\"\"PVE API client + guest command exec for MARION-IA-USA v0.11 work. Never prints secrets.\"\"\"\n3|import json, ssl, sys, urllib.request, urllib.parse, os\n4|\n5|PVE = 'https://10.0.20.100:8006'\n6|TICKET_FILE = '/home/jordatech/.llm-manager-v011/.pve_ticket'\n7|CTX = ssl.create_default_context(); CTX.check_hostname = False; CTX.verify_mode = ssl.CERT_NONE\n8|\n9|\n10|def _login():\n11|    creds = open('/home/jordatech/.llm-manager-v011/creds/pve_root.txt').read().split()\n12|    data = urllib.parse.urlencode({'username': f'{creds[0]}@pam', 'password': creds[1]}).encode()\n13|    req = urllib.request.Request(PVE + '/api2/json/access/ticket', data=data, method='POST')\n14|    r = json.load(urllib.request.urlopen(req, context=CTX, timeout=15))['data']\n15|    with open(TICKET_FILE, 'w') as f:\n16|        f.write(r['ticket'] + '\\n' + r['CSRFPreventionToken'])\n17|    os.chmod(TICKET_FILE, 0o600)\n18|\n19|\n20|def _tickets():\n21|    try:\n22|        lines = open(TICKET_FILE).read().split('\\n')\n23|        return lines[0], lines[1]\n24|    except Exception:\n25|        _login()\n26|        return _tickets()\n27|\n28|\n29|def api(method, path, body=None, retry=True):\n30|    ticket, csrf = _tickets()\n31|    headers = {'Cookie': f'PVEAuthCookie={ticket}'}\n32|    data = None\n33|    if body is not None:\n34|        enc = urllib.parse.urlencode(body).encode()\n35|        if method == 'GET':\n36|            # GET params must go in the querystring for PVE\n37|            path = path + ('&' if '?' in path else '?') + enc.decode()\n38|        else:\n39|            data = enc\n40|    if method != 'GET':\n41|        headers['CSRFPreventionToken'] = csrf\n42|    req = urllib.request.Request(PVE + path, data=data, headers=headers, method=method)\n43|    try:\n44|        resp = urllib.request.urlopen(req, context=CTX, timeout=180)\n45|        return json.load(resp)\n46|    except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:\n47|        if e.code == 401 and retry:\n48|            _login()\n49|            return api(method, path, body, retry=False)\n50|        raise\n51|\n52|\n53|def find_vm(node_hint=None, vmid=None, ip=None):\n54|    \"\"\"Locate a VM/LXC by vmid or current IP (via guest agent if possible).\"\"\"\n55|    vms = []\n56|    nodes = [n['node'] for n in api('GET', '/api2/json/nodes')['data']]\n57|    for node in nodes:\n58|        for vm in api('GET', f'/api2/json/nodes/{node}/qemu')['data']:\n59|            vms.append({'node': node, 'vmid': vm['vmid'], 'name': vm.get('name', ''), 'status': vm['status'], 'type': 'qemu'})\n60|        for ct in api('GET', f'/api2/json/nodes/{node}/lxc')['data']:\n61|            vms.append({'node': node, 'vmid': ct['vmid'], 'name': ct.get('name', ''), 'status': ct['status'], 'type': 'lxc'})\n62|    return vms\n63|\n64|\n65|def guest_exec(node, vmid, command, timeout=120):\n66|    \"\"\"Run command in guest via qemu-guest-agent, wait for completion, return (stdout, stderr, exitcode).\n67|    PVE expects 'command' as repeated form fields (arg encode_array).\"\"\"\n68|    # PVE array convention: repeat the parameter name with each value\n69|    # NOTE: 'timeout' is NOT an accepted param for agent/exec (400s); the exec returns a pid\n70|    parts = []\n71|    for c in command:\n72|        parts.append('command=' + urllib.parse.quote(c, safe=''))\n73|    body = '&'.join(parts).encode()\n74|    url = PVE + f'/api2/json/nodes/{node}/qemu/{vmid}/agent/exec'\n75|    ticket, csrf = _tickets()\n76|    headers = {'Cookie': f'PVEAuthCookie={ticket}', 'CSRFPreventionToken': csrf,\n77|               'Content-Type': 'application/x-www-form-urlencoded'}\n78|    req = urllib.request.Request(url, data=body, headers=headers, method='POST')\n79|    resp = urllib.request.urlopen(req, context=CTX, timeout=timeout + 30)\n80|    pdata = json.load(resp)['data']\n81|    pid = pdata['pid'] if isinstance(pdata, dict) else pdata\n82|    import time\n83|    deadline = time.time() + timeout\n84|    while time.time() < deadline:\n85|        time.sleep(2)\n86|        try:\n87|            st = api('GET', f'/api2/json/nodes/{node}/qemu/{vmid}/agent/exec-status', {'pid': pid})['data']\n88|        except Exception:\n89|            continue\n90|        if st.get('exited'):\n91|            return st.get('out-data', ''), st.get('err-data', ''), st.get('exitcode', -1)\n92|    return '', 'TIMEOUT', -1\n93|\n94|\n95|def lxc_exec(node, vmid, command):\n96|    r = api('POST', f'/api2/json/nodes/{node}/lxc/{vmid}/exec', {'command': json.dumps(command)})\n97|    # LXC exec via API returns after completion\n98|    return r\n99|\n100|\n101|if __name__ == '__main__':\n102|    cmd = sys.argv[1]\n103|    if cmd == 'find':\n104|        for v in find_vm():\n105|            print(f\"{v['vmid']:>5} {v['type']:<4} {v['status']:<7} {v['node']:<12} {v['name']}\")\n106|    elif cmd == 'exec':\n107|        node, vmid, command = sys.argv[2], sys.argv[3], sys.argv[4]\n108|        out, err, code = guest_exec(node, vmid, ['bash', '-lc', command])\n109|        print(out)\n110|        if err: print('STDERR:', err, file=sys.stderr)\n111|        sys.exit(0 if code == 0 else code)\n112|    elif cmd == 'api':\n113|        method, path = sys.argv[2], sys.argv[3]\n114|        body = json.loads(sys.argv[4]) if len(sys.argv) > 4 else None\n115|        print(json.dumps(api(method, path, body), indent=2)[:8000])\n116|", "total_lines": 115, "file_size": 4894, "truncated": false, "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}

# Startup Teams AI Agent Manager
## GLM 5.3 Flash Autonomous Implementation Plan — Version 3

**Document status:** V3 — V2 Agent Manager plan with mandatory MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager v0.11 closeout inserted as Phase 0  
**Primary executor:** GLM 5.3 Flash autonomous software-engineering agent  
**Execution mode:** Execute in strict order. Finish and verify Phase 0 before beginning any Agent Manager implementation work.  
**Primary objective:** First close the remaining LLM Manager v0.11 baseline work and formally establish the default model baseline. Then execute the complete Agent Manager V2 implementation plan on the same VM114 application/infrastructure.  

---

# MANDATORY EXECUTION ORDER

This document contains two sequential bodies of work. **Do not interleave them.**

1. **PHASE 0 — CLOSE MARION-IA-USA LLM MANAGER v0.11.** Execute the incorporated handoff below in the stated queue order. Treat the handoff's verified live state as the starting point, but re-verify before changing anything because runtime state can drift.
2. **PHASE 0 EXIT GATE.** Do not start Agent Manager work until:
   - the MIAM-00111 production topology is selected, implemented, verified, and documented;
   - the required remaining MIAM-00111 benchmark legs are complete;
   - the Phase 21 frontier-pool work is either completed or explicitly documented as BLOCKED/DEFERRED according to the handoff constraints;
   - `MARION-IA-USA-DEFAULT-MODEL-BASELINE-QUALIFICATION.md` is written;
   - the §24 final baseline acceptance gate is formally ticked and the baseline is marked **ESTABLISHED**;
   - the requested cleanup/validation items are completed or explicitly documented with rationale if optional/deferred;
   - LLM Manager services are healthy and the working Worker Bee service remains available.
3. **PHASE 1+ — AGENT MANAGER.** After the Phase 0 exit gate passes, continue directly into the full original Agent Manager V2 plan included after the handoff. Do not stop merely because Phase 0 completed.

## Phase 0 operating rules

- The uploaded handoff is the authoritative queue for remaining v0.11 work and explicitly supersedes the older §7 queue in `HANDOVER-v011.md`.
- Preserve the handoff's constraints exactly, especially: no RDMA/multi-node/SR-IOV work for Phase 21, do not disturb the Worker Bee rollout, preserve rollback presets, do not expose secrets, and use the established branch/repository authorization rules.
- Prefer the handoff recommendation of **TP2/DP3** for MIAM-00111 because the recorded evidence strongly favors it at the ~10-concurrent-coding-agent target. If live verification uncovers contradictory evidence, document it and make a reasoned decision rather than blindly applying a stale setting.
- Before destructive or service-changing operations, capture current state and ensure rollback is available.
- Reuse the verified access/deployment patterns in the handoff. In particular, use the established `pve.py` / `push.py` workflow rather than inventing a new remote-execution mechanism unless that path is broken.
- When benchmarking, honor the maintenance-state and single-benchmark-at-a-time rules so the recovery engine does not invalidate the run.
- Produce evidence as you go: command/config snapshots, test results, benchmark outputs, final service state, and report updates.
- **Autonomy rule:** ordinary technical choices are yours to make. Do not pause for permission unless credentials are missing, a required resource is unavailable, or an action would create material destructive/business risk.

---

## 3. WORK NOT COMPLETE — the next agent's queue (in order)

### 3.1 Phase 20 finale: pick the MIAM-00111 topology winner, implement, document ⭐ HIGHEST PRIORITY

Both candidate topologies were fully benchmarked (8/8 legs each, C1/C4/C8/C10 × 4K/16K, 32 prompts, 256 out). Verified results from `/root/bench_00111.log` (TP2/DP3) and `/root/bench_tp4.log` (TP4) on VM103:

| Leg | TP2/DP3 (3 replicas × 2 GPU, all 6 GPUs) | TP4 (1 replica × 4 GPU, 2 idle) |
|---|---|---|
| C1@4K | TTFT 8.6s · ITL 101ms · 2,635 tok/s | TTFT 56.4s · ITL 287ms · 957 tok/s |
| C1@16K | TTFT 77.1s · ITL 184ms · 2,599 | TTFT 225.7s · ITL 738ms · 1,061 |
| C4@4K | 18.5s · 94ms · 2,862 | 61.0s · 193ms · 1,225 |
| C4@16K | 84.1s · 181ms · 2,896 | 237.6s · 739ms · 1,061 |
| C8@4K | 19.6s · 91ms · 2,922 | 63.1s · 193ms · 1,225 |
| C8@16K | 85.8s · 154ms · 2,955 | 239.5s · 738ms · 1,062 |
| C10@4K | 24.9s · 112ms · **3,106** | 74.7s · 286ms · **1,167** |
| C10@16K | 110.6s · 165ms · 2,723 | 311.2s · 769ms · 1,034 |
| §20.3 coding eval | 6/6 tasks · 10/10 rubric · 313s | 6/6 tasks · 10/10 rubric · 211s |

**Evidence strongly favors TP2/DP3** at the §20.1 primary target (~10 concurrent coding agents): ~2.7× aggregate throughput, ~3× lower TTFT, ~2.5× lower ITL, all 6 GPUs used (better energy/token). TP4's only win is single-stream eval wall-time (211s vs 313s — sequential tasks).

## 6. Key bench gotchas (save the next agent hours)
- `vllm bench serve` needs `--tokenizer QuantTrio/Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-AWQ` (served name fails HF lookup); `--base-url http://127.0.0.1:8000 --endpoint /v1/chat/completions` (full URL in --endpoint silently zeroes all results); `HF_HOME=/opt/vllm-cache`.
- **Never run two bench jobs concurrently on one host** — saturation trips the manager health probe and the recovery engine restarts vLLM mid-bench (§K2 working as designed). Set host to MAINTENANCE *before* benchmarking (that's why host 1 is in MAINTENANCE now), restore SERVING after.
- Recovery engine ticks every 60s; `recovery_events` table is the audit trail.

# PHASE 1+ — ORIGINAL AI AGENT MANAGER V2 PLAN

The content below is the original V2 autonomous plan. Its implementation begins **only after Phase 0 passes**. Where its wording says to inspect the existing LLM Manager first, use the now-closed v0.11 baseline and its final qualification report as the authoritative starting baseline.


**Document status:** V2 — incorporates product-owner answers from 2026-09-13 and current open-source research  
**Primary executor:** GLM 5.3 Flash autonomous software-engineering agent  
**Execution mode:** Build, test, document, and make decisions autonomously. Do not stop for ordinary architectural ambiguity.  
**Primary objective:** Extend the existing **LLM Manager VM/application** into a unified infrastructure-management web application that can create, configure, access, monitor, and assign work to persistent AI agents on Proxmox.  
**First operational target:** A persistent **Hermes STEA** per employee, created with less than five minutes of human input and usable from a web interface within 15 minutes target / 30 minutes maximum.  
**Longer-term target:** Harness-neutral management of Hermes, Pi, Ruflo, and OpenClaw; Jira-linked projects and plans; Frontier-model supervisors; local-model worker agents; and a fleet UI that remains usable at 100–1,000 agents.

---

# 1. Executive directive to the implementation agent

You are not being asked to produce another architecture essay. You are being asked to **modify the existing LLM Manager system and ship a working prototype**.

Use these rules:

1. **Inspect first, then modify.** Read the existing LLM Manager repository, deployment method, services, auth, reverse proxy, database, and UI before changing anything.
2. **Do not create another management VM.** Agent Manager lives on the same VM as LLM Manager and appears as additional navigation tabs/routes in the same infrastructure-management experience.
3. **Do not rewrite working LLM Manager functionality.** Preserve it. Add Agent Manager as a modular subsystem.
4. **Make decisions.** If two approaches are reasonable, choose the simpler reversible approach, record the decision in an ADR, and continue.
5. **Do not ask the human to choose libraries or patterns that you can decide yourself.** Ask only when blocked by missing credentials, unavailable infrastructure, or a destructive decision with material business risk.
6. **Build the vertical slice first.** A single Hermes STEA that can be created and chatted with from the web is more valuable than ten unfinished abstractions.
7. **Keep every change reversible.** Back up the current application/database/configuration before schema or deployment changes. Use migrations. Use feature flags for incomplete adapters.
8. **Commit often.** Create small commits with clear messages after each working milestone.
9. **Measure provisioning.** Human interaction time, clone time, boot time, configuration time, harness readiness, and time-to-first-use must be captured independently.
10. **Leave the system healthier than you found it.** Add health checks, service documentation, backup instructions, and rollback steps.

---

# 2. Product vision

The LLM Manager and Agent Manager together become the control plane for Startup Teams' local AI infrastructure.

The application should eventually answer these questions from one browser:

- Which local models are online and how much capacity is available?
- Which AI agents exist?
- Which employee or business function owns each agent?
- Which harness and model is each agent using?
- Is an agent online, idle, working, blocked, waiting for review, or unhealthy?
- What project/Jira issue is it working on?
- What plan/instructions was it given?
- What did it change?
- What is waiting for human approval?
- Which agents need attention right now?
- Which agents are using external Frontier models and incurring cost?
- Can I message an agent right now from the browser?
- Can I create another persistent STEA without manually building a VM?

The product is an **operations console first**, not a wall of chat windows.

---

# 3. V2 fixed product decisions

These are decisions. Do not reopen them during implementation unless technically impossible.

## 3.1 Same VM and unified navigation

- **Do not create a separate Agent Manager VM.**
